## Analysis Summary

### Comparison Snapshot

| Metric | Value |
|---|---|
| Runs covered | `64` |
| Scored events | `59` |
| Excluded `non-AV` events | `333` |
| Excluded `accidental` events | `11` |
| Overall success rate | `52.5% (31/59)` |
| `unpudo` success rate | `53.6% (30/56)` |
| `unparking` success rate | `33.3% (1/3)` |
| Ownership / handover failures | `35.7% (10/28)` |
| Short AV-attempt failures | `28.6% (8/28)` |
| Successful events with no driver accel help | `100.0% (31/31)` |
| Successful events with route change found | `93.5% (29/31)` |
| Successful gear alignment at anchor | `100.0% (31/31)` |

### Failure Profile

- The scored subset contains `28` failures out of `59` scored events. The dominant effective failure types are `failed_to_unpudo` 57.1% (16/28), `not_av_owned` 17.9% (5/28), `completed_outside_av` 10.7% (3/28).
- Ownership loss and handover breakage are the main theme: `10` of `28` failures fall into `not_av_owned`, `completed_outside_av`, `driver_completed_maneuver`, or `interrupted_handover`.
- Route-change evidence inside failed events is `found` in `22`, `not found` in `0`, and `unclear` in `6` cases.
- Source disengagement labels inside failed events are `failed_to_unpudo` 57.1% (16/28), `uncategorised` 3.6% (1/28), `diversion` 3.6% (1/28); anything else is failing from DBW / ownership / gear evidence rather than an explicit source disengagement label.
- Event-type split: `unpudo` is stronger than `unparking` in this sample: 53.6% (30/56) versus 33.3% (1/3).

### Excluded Events

- `333` events are labelled `non-AV`: there is no AV-owned portion anywhere inside the detected event timeline, so they are mentioned for coverage but excluded from model scoring.
- `11` events are labelled `accidental`: AV participates for less than `2s`, so they are treated as accidental contact rather than a meaningful model attempt.

### Success Behavior

- Successful events are distributed as `30` `unpudo` and `1` `unparking`.
- On successful attempts, route change is recovered in `29` of `31` cases and driver accelerator help is absent in `31` of `31` cases.
- Gear state stays aligned at the anchor in `31` of `31` comparable passes. Planned-indicator alignment is `54.8% (17/31)`, and controller-indicator alignment is `54.8% (17/31)`.
- Typical successful timing is median `AV -> event start` `9.49s`, median `AV -> first motion` `10.04s`, and median AV-owned duration `226.74s`.
- Typical successful maneuver shape: common actual gear at the anchor is `DRIVE_POSITION_V2_DRIVE`, common actual indicator is `INDICATORS_STATE_V2_OFF`, and median max AV speed is `4.25 m/s`.

### Written Assessment

This card covers `403` detected events across `64` runs, but only `59` of them are scored AV-owned attempts. The remaining `333` `non-AV` and `11` `accidental` events are explicitly excluded from the success-rate denominator. Within the scored subset, the overall success rate is `52.5% (31/59)`. `unpudo` is stronger than `unparking` in this sample: 53.6% (30/56) versus 33.3% (1/3).

Across the scored failures, route-change evidence is `22` found / `0` not found / `6` unclear, so navigation context is usually present. The failure mix is led by `failed_to_unpudo` 57.1% (16/28), `not_av_owned` 17.9% (5/28), `completed_outside_av` 10.7% (3/28), while the excluded portion is mostly events with no AV-owned overlap or only sub-`2s` accidental AV contact rather than true scored failures.

When this model succeeds, the behavior is consistent: route change is recovered in `29` of `31` successful events, driver accelerator help is absent in `31` of `31`, and gear alignment holds in `31` of `31` comparable passes. The typical successful event starts moving about `10.04s` after AV engagement, reaches a median max AV speed of `4.25 m/s`, and most often begins in gear `DRIVE_POSITION_V2_DRIVE` with indicator state `INDICATORS_STATE_V2_OFF`.
